There hasn't been a time where I felt better or symptom-free for even two months.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have there been periods of at least two weeks when your depression became much worse and you also had several other symptoms like loss of interest, sleep or appetite changes, low energy, poor concentration, guilt, or thoughts of death?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced distinct two-week periods where things suddenly got much worse with thoughts of death or a loss of interest. Instead, my low mood, sleep issues, and feelings of worthlessness have just been relatively constant and continuous over the past two years and two months.\n\nDoctor: Along with the low mood, have you also had poor appetite or overeating, low energy, low self-esteem, poor concentration, or trouble making decisions during this time?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've definitely struggled with my appetite, either barely eating or overeating, and my self-esteem is extremely low because I constantly feel worthless and inferior to everyone else. On the other hand, I haven't really noticed having low energy, poor concentration, or any trouble making decisions.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually energetic or euphoric, needed much less sleep, talked more than usual, or were more impulsive than normal?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had any periods like that. I've never felt unusually energetic, euphoric, or needed less sleep. My mood has just been consistently low and depressed this entire time.\n\nDoctor: How much has this ongoing depression affected your work, school, relationships, or daily functioning?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm not really sure, and I haven't thought about it in those specific terms. Since I've felt this way for so long, it has just become my normal, so it's hard to say how much it has actually affected my work or relationships.\n\nDoctor: Can you give me one or two specific examples of how this low mood has affected your daily life, like your work, relationships, or ability to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: It is really hard to point to specific examples since this has just become my everyday reality. Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.
